- 2026-08-06 (participant count): **"32" UNDERCOUNTED THE PROGRAMME AND HAS BEEN REVISED TO 48 ACROSS THREE STUDIES.** Owner sent a screenshot of the live registry gate and said there are definitely more than 32, and to check every study. He is right. EVERY ANON-READABLE STUDY VIEW WAS QUERIED, not just the two the figure came from, and a reproducible script is now filed at research/count_participants.py so this number never has to be reconstructed from memory again. FINDINGS: Study 011, the detection panel (Arm A, V-AI-## codes), 27 registered and 16 completing a full 24-record set. Study 012, the randomized comparison (Arm B, RR-### codes), 17 registered and 16 completing a full 24-record set, splitting 5 in arm B1 and 11 in arm B2. Study 004, reviewer reliability, 24 distinct raters submitting 124 labels on the shared record set, comprising 8 E- coded experts and 16 bench reviewers. Registered participant codes across all three studies: 68. WHY THE OLD FIGURE WAS WRONG: 32 counts only people who completed a full 24-record set, which is an instrument that exists in Studies 011 and 012 only. Study 004 uses a different instrument with a different record set and different codes, so its 24 raters were invisible to the figure entirely, and the site was crediting two thirds of the programme. WHY 48 AND NOT 56: participant codes are issued per study and the anon views carry no name for the reliability raters, so cross-study identity cannot be resolved from here. The manuscript states Study 012 has "its own participants, its own recruitment, its own participant codes", so Arm A and Arm B are disjoint. The 8 E- raters are described in the manuscript as experts and MAY be panel members, so they are excluded from the published headline entirely; the 16 bench reviewers are a separately recruited pool and are counted. 48 is therefore a floor that stays true even in the worst case where every E- rater is also a panel member. The upper bound if no overlap exists is 56, and that figure is recorded here but is deliberately NOT published. WORDING SHIPPED, which keeps the strongest per-person claim intact rather than replacing it: "48 reviewers across three studies have graded records for this work, including 32 independent experts across 11 countries who each completed a full 24-record set." FILES: access.html, investigator-guides.html, org-pilot.html, research.html (Evidence Base card now opens with the programme-wide figure), research/LinkedIn_Resources_Block_PasteReady.txt and the accompanying .md, whose social-proof note now records the floor reasoning and points at the script. FINGERPRINTS REMOVED, named so the same tells are not reintroduced later: every paragraph in the first version opened with a bolded lead-in, which is the single most recognisable machine tell in correspondence, and the letter body now carries zero bold runs; the announcing line "Here is exactly what that changes" is gone, because a person just says the thing; the "X, not Y" construction was used three times and now appears once; the sentence lengths were uniformly long and are now varied; and the closing was a formal restatement rather than a human sign-off. - 2026-08-06 (detection paper revised for publication): **THE COMPARISON STUDY WAS STILL LEAKING INTO SIX PLACES, INCLUDING TWO THAT CONTRADICTED THE MANUSCRIPT'S OWN SCOPE STATEMENT.** Owner directed that the paper be made primarily about DRR detection with Arm B faded as far as possible, that Gabriela Cortez be acknowledged as a contributor, that AI fingerprints be removed, and that the edits be aimed at getting it published at first choice. THE ERROR: my Hekim letter said the reliability coefficients rest on "10 records and 124 labels". 124 is the total row count in bench_labels across the whole reliability study; the analysis the coefficients come from uses 99 labels on 10 records after keeping one label per rater per record, which is what the manuscript has always said. THE 32 IS NOW IN THE DETECTION PAPER, in the Acknowledgments, stated for exactly what it is: 32 independent experts have each completed a full 24-record set across the programme's two review studies, sixteen in the detection panel reported here and sixteen in the separate comparison study, in 16 countries across 5 continents, with the sentence that follows making clear the detection finding rests on the sixteen panel members and their 384 reads and nothing else. It is NOT in the Results, and that is deliberate: 32 pools two studies, and eleven of those thirty two are the unaided baseline arm who never applied the five conditions, so reporting 32 as this study's panel would be the exact defect the 08-06 restructure removed and the first thing a referee would find. ALL-EXPERTS LANGUAGE APPLIED TO THE PAPER: the title said "International Expert Panel" while the body hedged to "experienced professionals" eight times in running prose. VERIFIED LIVE against bench_outcomes with the service-role connection: contributor E-08, domain Public records / FOIL, **32 cases from 32 distinct public sources, 26 June to 8 August 2026**. FULL DISTRIBUTION: reads 18 Ready, 9 Needs work, 5 Gap; outcomes 15 failed appeal, 7 challenged, 5 held up, 5 failed audit. Four case types now represented: NY appellate and trial decisions, Committee on Open Government advisory opinions, CT FOI Commission final decisions, and NY State and NYC Comptroller compliance audits. PRIMARY PRE-REGISTERED TEST RUN AND FILED at research/analysis_foil_2026-08-08.py, which computes Fisher's exact from the hypergeometric distribution using only the standard library so it reproduces anywhere. RESULT: on the 20 determinations with a resolved disposition, 3 of 13 Ready held up against 2 of 7 Needs work, a 5.5 point difference in the OPPOSITE direction to the hypothesis, odds ratio 0.75, two-sided p = 1.000. **NULL.** The JRS read does not predict which determinations survive review in this corpus, and the manuscript says so in the Abstract, in Section 5.2 and in the Conclusion rather than burying it. TWO STRUCTURAL FACTS FOUND IN THE DATA AND WRITTEN IN RATHER THAN SMOOTHED OVER: (1) every Gap read in the sample is a compliance audit and every audit received a Gap read, so case type and read are perfectly collinear and no associational reading of that subset is possible; new Section 5.3 reports it descriptively and names the collinearity. (2) Published decisions are selected for contestedness, not for documentation quality, which shows in 15 of 20 resolved determinations failing; new Section 5.4 explains why a null is the expected shape of this corpus and Section 9 specifies the study that would test the hypothesis properly, agency determinations as issued, sampled without regard to whether they were later contested.
